Portrait composition is live: pads now 82×76 px in the lower third. Capturing the other two portrait sizes and the desktop view.

The tab went to the background and its chunked audio analysis stalled at 46% (a known background-tab issue, not a code change). I'll continue in a fresh foreground tab.

The browser has throttled the background tab (RAF suspended), so live gameplay can't advance. Let me force it back into an active state.

Rendering resumed once recording forced the tab active. Now the timing-tier session can run for real.




Stage 3.6 is implemented. The Stage 3.5 approach (open the FOV to 90° and push the pads deep) was the wrong lever — it shrank the pads as much as it fitted them. This version keeps the authored 70° FOV and instead treats the pad row as a *smaller row placed lower*, with everything in the judgement assembly sharing one uniform scale.

## What changed

`src/world/StageFraming.js` now returns one layout object for every consumer (pads, notes, impact FX, touch columns):

- **Portrait (aspect ≤ 0.8):** the pad row is scaled uniformly about the camera axis (pad geometry, lane centres, note size and note height all share one factor), its near edge stays at `PAD_NEAR_Z`, and the lane pitch tightens to 0.82× so each pad gets more screen width. Nothing is stretched — pads keep their exact authored proportions; the row is simply smaller and closer, which puts it in the lower third.
- **Wide (desktop):** the authored values, untouched — same FOV, same z row, same lane x, same notes, same HUD.
- Notes are judged inside the pad at the same scale, so they still land on the pad; the impact burst follows the judged note; the dancer silhouette stays proportionally above it all.
- Touch columns are cut at the midpoints between the *projected lane centres*, so each column contains exactly its own pad and columns tile the width with no overlap.
- Dancer panel: 72 px wide on portrait (was 84), top-left — the pads now own the bottom 25%, so the two no longer share any space.

## Measurements (measured, not estimated — `node qa/stage36-layout-check.mjs`, exit 0)

| Viewport | Pad (near edge) | Far edge | Gap | Edge margin | Row vertical band | Touch zone | 
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 360×800 | **76 × 70.4 px** | 38.3 px | 10.0 px | 13.1 px | 76.3 – 85.1 % | 86–94 × 158 px |
| 390×844 | **82.3 × 75.8 px** | 41.3 px | 10.8 px | 14.2 px | 76.1 – 85.1 % | 93–102 × 168 px |
| 430×932 | **90.8 × 83.6 px** | 45.5 px | 11.9 px | 15.7 px | 76.1 – 85.1 % | 103–112 × 185 px |
| 1280×720 | 204.3 × 127.4 px | 70 px | 77.5 px | 115.2 px | 67.1 – 84.8 % | unchanged |
| 1920×1080 | 306.4 × 191.2 px | 105 px | 116.2 px | 172.8 px | 67.1 – 84.8 % | unchanged |

Stage 3.5 → 3.6 at 390×844: pad **72 × 50 px at 40.7 % of the height → 82.3 × 75.8 px at 76.1 – 85.1 %**. Roughly 1.7× the area, and it moved from mid-frame to the lower third.

![Portrait gameplay at 390x844](screenshots/stage36-390x844.png)

The four zones are contiguous lane columns and independently usable (measured in the live page at 390×844: lane 0 at x 0–102, lane 1 at 102–195, lane 2 at 195–288, lane 3 at 288–390, all 262 px tall).

## Desktop

Bit-identical to Stage 3.5: FOV 70, `padScale 1`, pads at x ±3/±1, `z` 7.2→3.8, hit depth 0, note scale 1, note height 1.05. Verified both numerically and from a fresh 1280×720 capture.

![Desktop, unchanged](screenshots/stage36-1280x720.png)

## Regression results

Passed, measured live after the change:

- Four-lane mapping: each touch zone emits only its own lane, lights only its own pad (pulse 0.35 on the pressed lane, zero elsewhere), and clears on release.
- Duplicate `pointerdown` (same pointerId) emits the lane twice but scores nothing the second time.
- Multi-touch: two outer zones pressed simultaneously → both pressed, lane events `[0, 3]`.
- Release outside the zone: pressed state cleared (window-level release net).
- Containment: `touch-action: none`, `user-select: none`, body overflow hidden, `scrollY 0`.
- Restart path: loading → intro → playing with the portrait layout applied (used repeatedly).
- Console: only Vite dev logs — no WebGL warnings, no runtime errors.
- Build: `npm run build` exit 0 — 1,075.98 kB JS (294.95 kB gzip), 64.71 kB CSS.

**Not verified, and why:** the browser suspended the page (`document.hidden`, RAF not firing), so the audio clock ran at ~0.16× wall time. That killed the controlled full-song timing sweep, pause/resume and seek timing deltas, the FPS benchmark, and the gameplay video (the recorder captured only the results screen — which did show all four tiers resolving: 1 perfect / 3 great / 2 good / 15 miss). Those need a visible browser window; say the word and I'll re-run them and capture a real clip.

## Remaining limitations

- **No physical device.** Everything ran in the local Chromium viewport with emulated sizes; touch input was synthetic pointer events.
- **Pad width has a hard ceiling.** Four pads across a 390 px screen can be at most ~97 px each; with the authored 1.45 m pad width against a 2.0 m lane pitch, pad : gap is fixed at 1.45 : 0.55, which caps a pad at ~21–24 % of the screen width. That is why this is 82 px, not 120 px. Going bigger requires portrait-specific pad proportions (wider pads, thinner gaps) — a design change I did not make unilaterally.
- Pad height is set by the floor-plane foreshortening at 70° FOV; the only way to make them relatively taller is a steeper (more top-down) portrait camera, which would visibly change the tunnel perspective.

**Files changed:** `src/world/StageFraming.js`, `src/world/DancePads.js`, `src/objects/NoteManager.js`, `src/core/Game.js`, `src/game/Gameplay.js`, `src/ui/UIManager.js`, `src/styles/main.css`, plus new `qa/stage36-layout-check.mjs` and `qa-artifacts/stage36-{layout,validation}.json`.

## 2. Những gì mình đồng ý

- Giữ FOV 70° thay vì mở rộng quá mức.
- Scale đồng nhất, không bóp méo hình thang.
- Giữ desktop nguyên bản.
- Touch zones khớp với vị trí pad.

## 3. Nhưng còn một vấn đề cần quyết định

A. Giữ nguyên tỷ lệ 3D hiện tại

- Pads đúng hình học.

  An toàn nhất

- Tăng chiều rộng pads.
- Giảm khoảng trống giữa pads.
- Giữ bốn lane riêng biệt.
- Desktop không thay đổi.
- Có thể phải điều chỉnh hình học riêng cho portrait.
  Ưu tiên trải nghiệm chơi

Cá nhân mình chọn B, nhưng chỉ khi giữ được phối cảnh đẹp và bốn pads không chồng lấn.

## 4. Còn một việc bắt buộc: kiểm thử lại gameplay

OpenCode đã nói rõ trình duyệt bị background throttle nên chưa kiểm tra được:

- Pause/resume.
- Seek/restart.
- FPS thực tế.
- Gameplay video.
- Timing sweep liên tục.

Mình muốn hoàn thành những kiểm thử này trước khi đóng Stage 3.6.

|---|---|

|---|---|---|---|---|

| Backward | 80.86 s | 70.86 s | 71.17 s | +0.50 s / 0.50 s |

## Restart from the beginning

From 85.54 s → **0.00 s**, through results → loading → intro → playing. Repeated several times during the session.

## Timing tiers (medium: perfect ≤ 80 ms, great ≤ 173.6 ms, good ≤ 280 ms)

Real `KeyboardEvent`s dispatched through `InputManager` at a controlled offset from each note:

| Offset | Judgement |
|---|---|
| 0 ms | **perfect** |
| 120 ms | **great** |
| 220 ms | **good** |
| 420 ms | no judgement — outside the window, note counted as **miss** |

## Four-lane mapping

- Keyboard: `KeyA → 0`, `KeyS → 1`, `KeyD → 2`, `KeyF → 3` — one lane event each, none crossed.
- Touch: each of the four zones emitted only its own lane, reported `pressed` on pointerdown and released on pointerup.

## FPS and frame time (active gameplay)

| Viewport | FPS | Avg frame | p95 | Max | Draw calls | Triangles | Heap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 390×844 portrait | 60.38 | 16.561 ms | 16.8 ms | 16.9 ms | 166 | 14,914 | 39.05 MB |
| 1280×720 desktop | 60.44 | 16.544 ms | 16.8 ms | 16.9 ms | 168 | 15,206 | — |

24 shader programs, 16 textures, no quality reduction applied.

## Console and WebGL

`gl.getError()` → **null**, broken programs → **0**, app errors → **0**. The console after a clean reload contains only Vite dev logs.

One transparency note: mid-session the console did show `[EventBus] handler for "game:judge" threw` errors — those came from a stale listener **my own test harness** had attached to `window.__TIER` (the stack points at `<anonymous>`, not app code). After removing the instrumentation and reloading, the session above ran clean. The app's EventBus caught and logged the foreign handler instead of crashing.

## Deliverables

- **Gameplay recording with audio:** `screenshots/stage36-gameplay.mp4` — 24 s, 480×802, H.264 + AAC, audio muxed from the live song position 36.16 s. Verified by extracting a frame: score 70,681, combo 151, four pads lit at the bottom.
- **Portrait 390×844:** `screenshots/stage36-390x844.png` — all four pads visible in the lower third, combo 404.
- **Desktop 1280×720:** `screenshots/stage36-1280x720.png` — wide mode, FOV 70, `padScale 1`, lanes at ±3/±1, hit depth 0, i.e. the authored composition.
- **Data:** `qa-artifacts/stage36-validation.json` (now includes the full live-verification block), `qa-artifacts/stage36-layout.json`.
- **Build:** `npm run build` exit 0 — 1,075.98 kB JS (294.95 kB gzip), 64.71 kB CSS.

Portrait:

![Portrait 390x844](screenshots/stage36-390x844.png)

Desktop:

![Desktop 1280x720](screenshots/stage36-1280x720.png)

## Limitations

- **No physical device.** Everything ran in this Chromium instance with emulated viewport sizes; touch was synthetic `PointerEvent`s and keys were synthetic `KeyboardEvent`s. Nobody has tapped a real phone screen.
- Pad width is at its geometric ceiling (82.3 px of 390 px): four pads across with the authored 1.45 m pad width and 2.0 m lane pitch allow ~21–24 % of screen width per pad. Anything wider needs portrait-specific pad proportions.
- Pad height comes from floor-plane foreshortening at 70°; making them relatively taller needs a steeper portrait camera, which would change the tunnel perspective you asked me to preserve.
- The tab must stay foregrounded for live runs — when it was backgrounded earlier, Chromium suspended the render loop and the audio clock dropped to ~0.16× wall time, which is what interrupted the previous evaluation.

| --- | --- |

| 120 ms | Great |

| 420 ms | Miss |

60 FPS ổn định ở cả portrait và desktop, không cần giảm chất lượng đồ họa.

Đây là tín hiệu tích cực cho bước tiếp theo.

- Độ trễ cảm ứng thực tế.
- Hiệu năng trên GPU điện thoại cấu hình thấp.
- Khả năng duy trì 60 FPS khi máy nóng.

Ngoài ra, pads rộng 82.3 px ở màn hình 390 px vẫn chưa phải kích thước lý tưởng nhất.

Mình chưa khuyên thay đổi tiếp vì có thể ảnh hưởng đến thiết kế 3D đã ổn định.

# NEON PULSE – STAGE 4: GAMEPLAY EXPERIENCE & PRODUCT POLISH

Stage 1 through Stage 3.6 are now considered the accepted technical baseline.

The current build has:

- Geometric neon tunnel.
- Reflective floor.
- Four independent dance pads.
- White dancer silhouette.
- Music-driven choreography.
- Four-lane rhythm gameplay.
- Scoring, combo and timing judgements.
- Responsive portrait composition.
- Stable audio synchronization.

## IMPORTANT RULES

Do not rewrite existing systems.

Do not redesign the established Stage 1 visual environment.

Do not modify the accepted four-lane architecture.

Preserve the current camera and desktop composition.

## PHASE 1 – AUDIT

Inspect the entire application and identify opportunities to improve:

1. Gameplay feedback and visual effects.
2. Perfect/Great/Good/Miss presentation.
3. Results screen.
4. Music selection experience.
5. Difficulty progression.
6. Dance choreography responsiveness.
7. Audio analysis and chart quality.
8. Mobile usability.

## PHASE 2 – PROPOSAL

Before writing code, provide:

- Current implementation status.
- Weaknesses in the existing UX.
- Proposed improvements.
- Files that would need modification.
- Regression risks.
- A staged implementation plan.

Do not modify any files during this audit.

Wait for approval before implementation.

The goal is to transform NEON PULSE from a technically working rhythm game into a polished, enjoyable product without sacrificing its established visual identity.

Đánh giá của mình: Stage 3.6 đã hoàn thành tốt mục tiêu kỹ thuật. Mình muốn bảo vệ những gì đã xây dựng được, tránh để OpenCode liên tục sửa camera, tunnel và gameplay chỉ vì muốn cải thiện một vài chi tiết nhỏ.

Đó chính là mục tiêu của Stage 4.

I audited the code and probed the running build read-only — **no files were modified**. Here is what I found and what I propose.

# Phase 1 — Audit

## What exists and works (verified)

| Area | Status |
|---|---|
| Four-lane core | 4 pads ↔ 4 lanes, 1:1, A/S/D/F + arrows + digits/numpad + touch |
| Scoring | Combo → ×8 multiplier, weighted accuracy, health 100, 7/hit miss damage, 18/bomb, rank S+/S/A/B/C/D |
| Timing | Perfect/Great/Good/Miss computed to the millisecond against `MusicPlayer.getTime()` |
| Analysis | Clementi: BPM 175 (confidence 0.98), 396 kicks, 416 snares, 219 hats |
| Structure | Sections (intro/verse/build-up/drop/breakdown/outro) + climax/drop detection |
| FX | Impact bursts, pad flash, camera punch, screen shake, bloom, theme crossfades, 12 palettes |
| Dancer | 7 motion families, 12-beat cycle, phrase arc, section bias, kick/snare accents |
| Perf | 60.4 fps, 16.5 ms avg, 24 shader programs, 0 WebGL errors |
| Responsive | Portrait composition accepted (pads 82.3×75.8 px at 390×844) |

## Weaknesses, with evidence

**1. The judgement system is computed but never shown.** In the live DOM right now: `.judgement` → `display:none`, `.hud__health` → `display:none`, `.hud__banner` → `display:none`, `.hud__intro` → `display:none`, `.hud__track-name` → `display:none`, `.hud__left-bottom` → `display:none`, `.lanes` → `display:none`. A block in `main.css` (~line 2763, "NEON TUNNEL REDESIGN") hides them all with `!important`. The result: **no PERFECT / GREAT / GOOD / MISS text ever appears**, there is no health bar (death is unexplained), no drop banner, no track title, and no mouse lane buttons. All of `showJudgement()`, `setSection()` and the health CSS are wired but invisible.

**2. Charts are not reproducible.** Two generations from the *same* analysis diverged at note 0 and produced **403 vs 408 notes** — `BeatMapGenerator` uses `Math.random()` for pattern pick, hat sampling and bomb lane/roll. Same track + difficulty = a different chart every run, so scores can't be compared and "retry this chart" is impossible.

**3. The difficulty ladder barely exists on dense music.**

| Difficulty | Notes | Notes/s | Longest silence |
|---|---|---|---|
| EASY | 198 | 1.84 | 1.05 s |
| NORMAL | 407 | 3.79 | 0.84 s |
| HARD | 411 | 3.82 | 0.84 s |
| EXPERT | 420 | 3.91 | 0.84 s |

Normal → Expert is a 3 % density change; only the hit windows (280→170 ms) and scroll speed differ. Because notes are kick-driven with `kickEvery = 1`, density saturates at the kick rate and Hard/Expert have nothing left to add.

**4. The "dodge the red" mechanic is effectively absent.** 67 snares exceeded the 0.9 strength gate, yet the charts contain **4 obstacles** (medium), 4 (hard), 2 (easy) in a 107 s song. The lane-free check (`|e.time − snare.time| < 0.3` on every lane) almost never passes on a 3.8 note/s chart, so hazards get dropped.

**5. No persistence and no progression loop.** Only `Settings` and `Studio` touch `localStorage`. There is no per-track best score, rank, history or "new record" — the results screen is a dead end that always looks the same.

**6. Results screen is thin.** Rank + score + accuracy + max combo + four counts + Retry/Change/Menu. Bombs are folded into the MISS number. No comparison to your best, no delta, no accuracy breakdown, no suggestion of what to try next, no quick difficulty switch.

**7. Music selection is a 3-screen funnel with no memory.** Menu → Library → Difficulty → song. No continue/recent/favourites, no "last played", no BPM or length hint until the difficulty screen, no recommended difficulty, and Jamendo requiring a key means the library is mostly an upload box plus the demo track.

**8. Dancer reactivity is coarse.** `Game` calls `silhouette.accent(strength, success)` from the judgement alone — it never learns *which lane* was hit, so left/right hits look identical, combo tiers don't change amplitude, and there is no recovery beat after a miss. The choreography itself is good and correctly time-derived.

**9. Mobile gaps.** Touch zones are invisible (no finger-down indication on the pad itself), haptics fire only on perfect/bomb, no fullscreen/orientation affordance, and the three most useful feedback elements (judgement, health, track title) are the ones hidden by CSS.

# Phase 2 — Proposal

## Design guardrails

Additive only: no camera, tunnel, floor, pad geometry/colour, four-lane mapping, scoring math, timing windows or audio-sync changes. New feedback must read as part of the minimal HUD language (thin type, mono, colour from the active palette).

## Proposed improvements

**A. Give the judgement system a face (highest impact, lowest risk)**
- A compact judgement rail above the pad band: `PERFECT` / `GREAT` / `GOOD` / `MISS` with the delta in ms, colour-coded from `JUDGEMENTS`, 450 ms lifetime, stacked so rapid hits read.
- Replace the hidden bar with a thin peripheral health edge (bottom-left corner glow) that desaturates and pulses red under 30 % — no new panel, keeps the frame clean.
- Restore the drop banner and "NOW PLAYING" card, restyled to the current minimal type scale.

**B. Deterministic charts**
- Seeded PRNG keyed by `hash(track.id + difficulty + chartVersion)`; every `Math.random()` in the generator goes through it.
- Guarantees identical charts per track/difficulty, which is the prerequisite for best scores, fair retries and any leaderboard later.

**C. A real difficulty ladder**
- Per-difficulty notes-per-second targets instead of "the kick rate": EASY ≤ 1.6, NORMAL ≤ 2.6, HARD ≤ 3.4, EXPERT ≤ 4.4, enforced by the spacing pass.
- NORMAL thins kicks (every 2nd on dense tracks) so the step from EASY is meaningful; EXPERT adds 16th off-beats and 2-note chords only in free lanes.
- Place hazards **before** the note fill and reserve their lane ±0.3 s, so obstacles land at a designed rate (≈1 per 12–18 s) instead of vanishing.

**D. Results and progression**
- Persistent store `neon-pulse:scores` → best score, best rank, max combo, plays per `(trackId, difficulty)`.
- Results screen adds: NEW RECORD badge + delta vs best, accuracy ring, judgement distribution bar, "next step" hint (e.g. *"3.2 % from S — try HARD"*), and Retry that can switch difficulty in place.
- Fails show how far the run got vs the chart (`78 % of the song`).

**E. Dancer responsiveness**
- Pass `lane` into `silhouette.accent` → mirror lean (left hits lean left), combo tier scales amplitude (1–4), miss adds a recoil and a 1-beat recovery, drop switches motion family for its duration. Still pure functions of song time, so seek/restart stays deterministic.

**F. Music selection**
- Continue-last-track card at the top of the library, favourites star, recent list (all local).
- Collapse Library + Difficulty into one screen: track list on the left, difficulty + chart density preview on the right.
- Show detected BPM/duration on the track row; recommend a difficulty from the stored best.

**G. Mobile polish**
- Visible lane press indication on the pad itself (edge flare in the lane colour), haptics on every judgement (short for good, double for miss), optional fullscreen toggle in the pause panel, and a one-time portrait hint.

## Files that would change

| Stage | Files |
|---|---|
| A | `src/styles/main.css`, `src/ui/UIManager.js`, `src/ui/templates.js` |
| B | `src/audio/BeatMapGenerator.js`, `src/utils/math.js` (hash/PRNG helper) |
| C | `src/audio/BeatMapGenerator.js` |
| D | new `src/game/ScoreStore.js`, `src/ui/UIManager.js`, `src/ui/templates.js`, `src/styles/main.css`, `src/core/Game.js` (pass track/difficulty into results) |
| E | `src/game/DanceSilhouette.js`, `src/core/Game.js` |
| F | `src/ui/templates.js`, `src/ui/UIManager.js`, `src/music/MusicLibrary.js`, `src/styles/main.css` |
| G | `src/ui/UIManager.js`, `src/styles/main.css`, `src/core/Settings.js` |

Untouched by all of it: `world/*` (except nothing), `core/CameraRig.js`, `StageFraming.js`, `DancePads.js`, `NoteManager.js`, `Scoring.js`, `MusicPlayer.js`, `AudioManager.js`.

## Regression risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Unhiding HUD elements collides with the pad band on portrait | Position judgement rail from the same framing projection as the touch band; verify at 360/390/430 that it never overlaps pads or the dancer panel |
| New HUD text competes with the minimal look | Mono type ≤ 11 px, palette colours, 450 ms life, opacity cap |
| Seeded charts change every existing chart | Intentional; bump `chartVersion` and note it in the report. Existing QA JSON expectations must be regenerated |
| Difficulty retuning makes a track too easy/hard | Enforce NPS targets in `enforceSpacing` and verify counts across all four QA tracks |
| Persistence breaks in private mode | try/catch, memory fallback, namespaced key |
| Dancer changes break seek determinism | Keep every value a function of `time`, `combo`, `lane` only — no accumulated state |

## Staged plan

| Stage | Scope | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| **4.1** | Judgement rail, health edge, banners, track title | Judgement text visible for all four tiers at 390/1280; no overlap with pads; 60 fps held; no visual change to the 3D scene |
| **4.2** | Seeded charts + difficulty NPS ladder + hazard placement | Same seed → byte-identical charts; NPS within targets for all 4 difficulties; ≥ 6 hazards per track |
| **4.3** | Score store + results screen upgrade | NEW RECORD appears on improvement; best/rank persist across reloads; delta correct |
| **4.4** | Dancer lane/combo reactivity | Left/right hits produce mirrored lean; combo tiers scale amplitude; seek to any time reproduces the same pose |
| **4.5** | Library/selection + mobile polish | One-screen pick flow; continue card; visible pad press; haptics on all judgements |

I'd start with **4.1** — it is CSS/DOM only, touches no gameplay logic, and fixes the single biggest experience gap (the game currently never tells you how you hit a note).

Nothing is implemented yet. Tell me to proceed with 4.1, approve the whole ladder, or cut/reshape any stage before I write code.


Mình đã đọc toàn bộ bản audit. Và mình đồng ý với kết luận của OpenCode: nên bắt đầu Stage 4.1 trước, chưa nên triển khai tất cả cùng lúc.

## 1. Đánh giá bản audit

Implementing Stage 4.1 — feedback layer only. First, the current styles and the UI methods I'll be reusing.
















Stage 4.1 is implemented and verified. The feedback layer existed in the DOM but was hidden by a blanket `display: none !important` block from the Stage 3.6 redesign — this stage unhides and restyles four of those elements only.

## What changed

**`src/styles/main.css`** — removed `.judgement`, `.hud__health`, `.hud__banner`, `.hud__track-name` from the hidden-HUD block (`.hud__times`, `.hud__chip--diff`, `.hud__left-bottom`, `.hud__intro`, `.lanes` stay hidden), then appended a Stage 4.1 block:
- **Judgement rail** moved from 41% to 58% height (60–61% portrait) so it clears the pad band, with `translate` used for centring instead of `transform` so GSAP's scale/pop can't fight the centring. Tones: PERFECT pure white with white+cyan glow, GREAT `#7ef1ff`, GOOD `#ffd166`, MISS `#ff4d6d`. One ghost slot above the text.
- **Health** shrunk from 5px to a 2px hairline whose fill opacity is `0.45 + health × 0.55`, themed gradient glow, and a `healthWarning` keyframe (red gradient, 1.1s pulse) under 30%.
- **Banner** moved from `bottom: 21%` (which sat inside the portrait pad band) to 41% top / 47–50% portrait.
- **Track label** single-line, `min(46vw, 340px)` desktop / `min(58vw, 250px)` portrait, ellipsis.

**`src/ui/UIManager.js`** — `showJudgement` now writes the timing offset (`12ms EARLY` / `108ms LATE` / `ON TIME`), pushes the previous message into a single fading ghost (so rapid hits never stack two full-size messages), and uses a shorter 0.71s timeline. `updateHUD` drives the `--health` variable and `.is-low` class. `showBanner` dedupes identical copy inside 500ms and animates y; new `hideBanner()`/`hideJudgement()` are called from `showPause()`. `showGame` writes the full track name into `title` as well as the visible label.

**`src/ui/templates.js`** — one added `judgement__ghost` span.

Untouched: tunnel, floor, camera, pads, lane mapping, `BeatMapGenerator`, `Scoring.js`, audio engine, `DanceChoreography`.

- No physical device testing: every press was a synthetic `KeyboardEvent`, and layout was verified at emulated viewport sizes only.

Tiếp tục nhé\! Mình sẽ chuyển sang Stage 4.2 — Deterministic Charts & Difficulty Progression.

Mục tiêu là cải thiện hệ thống tạo chart mà không phá những phần đã hoàn thiện ở Stage 1–4.1.

### Phạm vi triển khai

1. Deterministic chart

- Seeded PRNG dựa trên `track.id + difficulty + chartVersion`.
- Loại bỏ mọi `Math.random()` không kiểm soát trong `BeatMapGenerator.js`.
- Cùng bài nhạc, cùng difficulty phải tạo chart giống hệt nhau.
- Retry không thay đổi vị trí note hoặc hazard.

2. Phân cấp độ khó

| Difficulty | Mục tiêu notes/s |
| --- | --- |
| EASY | ≤ 1.6 |
| NORMAL | ≤ 2.6 |
| HARD | ≤ 3.4 |
| EXPERT | ≤ 4.4 |

- EASY: nhịp đơn, ít chuyển lane.
- NORMAL: thêm nhịp phụ.
- HARD: syncopation và chuyển lane nhanh hơn.
- EXPERT: off-beat, chord và pattern phức tạp.

3. Hazard system

- Đặt hazard trước khi fill note.
- Dành khoảng trống an toàn quanh hazard.
- Mục tiêu khoảng 1 hazard mỗi 12–18 giây.
- Không để hazard xuất hiện ở vị trí bất khả thi.

4. Regression tests

- So sánh chart bằng hash.
- Kiểm tra NPS từng difficulty.
- Kiểm tra số hazard.
- Kiểm tra lane mapping 0–3.
- Kiểm tra không trùng note/hazard.
- Build và kiểm tra gameplay với audio thật.

### Những phần tuyệt đối giữ nguyên

- Stage 1 tunnel, camera, floor và pads.
- Stage 2.5 choreography.
- Stage 3 four-lane mapping.
- Stage 3.6 portrait framing.
- Stage 4.1 judgement rail và HUD.
- Scoring, combo, timing windows và audio synchronization.

Bạn mở lại workspace/project hoặc gửi các file `BeatMapGenerator.js`, `config.js` và QA harness hiện tại nhé. Mình sẽ bắt đầu đọc code rồi triển khai trực tiếp, không giả lập kết quả hay báo build thành công khi chưa thực sự chạy.

# Stage 4.2 — Deterministic Charts & Difficulty Progression

Continue development from the accepted Stage 4.1 baseline.

Your task is to implement Stage 4.2 directly in the existing codebase. Do not rewrite existing systems or redesign the game.

## 1\. First: Audit the Existing Code

Before editing, inspect:

- `src/audio/BeatMapGenerator.js`
- `src/config.js`
- Existing chart generation and spacing logic
- Current obstacle/bomb generation
- Existing QA harnesses and validation artifacts
- How track IDs, BPM, difficulty and chart data are passed through the game

Understand the current implementation before making changes.

Do not assume the previous audit report is perfectly accurate. Verify everything against the actual source code.

## 2\. Deterministic Chart Generation

Implement a seeded PRNG.

Seed should be derived from:

`track.id + difficulty + chartVersion`

Requirements:

- Replace uncontrolled `Math.random()` calls inside chart generation.
- All random pattern selection, hat sampling, lane selection and bomb placement must use the seeded generator.
- Identical track + difficulty + chart version must produce byte-identical chart data.
- Different difficulties should produce distinct charts.
- Chart generation must not depend on playback state, frame rate or previous runs.
- Keep the implementation deterministic across reloads and retries.

Introduce a chart version constant so future chart-generation changes can be tracked explicitly.

## 3\. Meaningful Difficulty Progression

Target approximate note densities:

| Difficulty | Target maximum NPS |
| --- | --- |
| EASY | 1.6 |
| NORMAL | 2.6 |
| HARD | 3.4 |
| EXPERT | 4.4 |

Implement genuinely different chart structures:

- EASY: simpler kick-driven patterns, fewer lane transitions and longer breathing spaces.
- NORMAL: additional rhythmic subdivisions and moderate variation.
- HARD: stronger syncopation, faster lane transitions and more complex patterns.
- EXPERT: off-beat patterns, occasional two-note chords and more demanding combinations.

Do not achieve difficulty differences merely by changing hit windows or scroll speed.

Preserve musical alignment and avoid artificial note spam.

Treat NPS targets as design goals, not permission to destroy important musical accents. Document any unavoidable deviations.

## 4\. Hazard / Bomb Placement

Fix the current hazard-generation bottleneck.

Requirements:

- Place hazards before normal note filling.
- Reserve safe time windows around hazards.
- Avoid note/hazard collisions.
- Preserve playable lanes and reasonable reaction time.
- Target approximately one hazard every 12–18 seconds where the music supports it.
- Never force hazards into unsuitable musical sections simply to meet a numerical quota.

Keep hazard behavior and scoring unchanged.

## 5\. Regression Testing

Create or extend automated QA tests.

Verify:

1. Same seed generates identical chart data.
2. Reload/retry produces identical charts.
3. Different difficulties produce meaningfully different patterns.
4. All notes use valid lanes 0–3.
5. No invalid timestamps or NaN values.
6. No impossible note/hazard overlaps.
7. Hazard spacing is reasonable.
8. NPS distribution is measured for all four difficulties.
9. Existing scoring and judgement behavior remains unchanged.
10. Existing Stage 4.1 feedback remains functional.

Test with available real audio fixtures, especially:

- Clementi
- Chopin
- Brahms

Also test procedural or synthetic analysis inputs where useful, clearly distinguishing synthetic validation from real-audio testing.

## 6\. Strict Preservation Rules

- Neon tunnel

- DanceChoreography
- DanceSilhouette

- Health indicator
- Section banners
- Track title HUD
- Scoring formulas
- Timing windows
- Audio playback and synchronization

Only touch additional files when technically necessary.

## 7\. Build and Verification

Run:

- Existing QA tests
- New Stage 4.2 tests
- `npm run build`

Report:

- Note count per difficulty and track
- NPS per difficulty
- Hazard count and spacing
- Determinism/hash comparison results
- Invalid lane/collision counts
- Build result
- Any remaining limitations

Do not fabricate test results. If a test cannot run, state why.

## What changed

**`src/audio/BeatMapGenerator.js`** (the only gameplay file rewritten):
- `CHART_VERSION = 2`; seed key `track.id::difficulty::v2` via `chartSeedKey()`, fed into `createSeededRandom()` (xmur3 hash + mulberry32). Every former `Math.random()` site (pattern pick, hat sampling, lane choice, bomb placement) now draws from that stream. `generatedAt: Date.now()` was removed so chart objects are byte-identical between runs.
- Per-difficulty shaping fields kept separate from the frozen timing fields: `maxNps` (1.6 / 2.6 / 3.4 / 4.4), `grid`, `beatSlots`, `fillChance`, `onsetWindow`, `chordChance`, `restBars`, `hazardGap`, `diversify`. `hitWindow`, `perfectWindow`, `minGap`, `speed`, colours and labels are untouched.
- Pipeline: hazards planned first (strong-snare only, per-difficulty 12–26 s gaps, reserved windows) → kick layer → subdivision grid (downbeats belong to kicks) → `collapseSimultaneous` (an accent at 1.400 s beats a grid slot at 1.375 s instead of being discarded) → per-lane spacing → expert chords → floor fill → hazard lane resolution (move or drop, never force) → two-window density governor (`1.02 s`/`2.02 s` so an inclusive 1 s measurement can never read one note over the cap) → final lane-flow repair so hard/expert never repeat a lane when a free one exists.

**`src/core/Game.js`, `src/studio/Studio.js`, `src/studio/StudioStage.js`**: pass `trackId` into both `generate()` and `generateProcedural()`. Nothing else in those files changed (verified by mtime: `main.css`, `UIManager.js`, `templates.js` are untouched from Stage 4.1).

**`qa/stage42-chart-check.mjs`** + `qa-artifacts/analysis/{clementi,chopin,brahms}.json` (real, unrounded in-app analyses) + `qa-artifacts/stage42-chart-check.json`.

Examples: Clementi expert `9ae8dac441e85e7f`, Chopin expert `ca55f50f9f591bb5`, Brahms expert `6e3dd5d939030d39`, synthetic expert `b0aeca5871fe510a`. Seed contract verified as `synthetic-120bpm::hard::v2`; explicit seed, different track id and different difficulty all produce different charts.

Invalid data counts across all 16 charts: **0** invalid lanes, **0** invalid/NaN timestamps, **0** unsorted, **0** duplicate time+lane pairs, **0** note/hazard collisions, **0** per-lane spacing violations. Preserved behaviour re-asserted: timing windows easy 0.34/0.10, medium 0.28/0.08, hard 0.21/0.06, expert 0.17/0.052; scoring smoke test 120/90/210 score/93 health/60 % accuracy/rank D; bomb colour `#ff2d55`.

## Browser verification

## Limitations (honest list)

- The browser tab is occluded (`document.hidden: true`), so the app auto-pauses: I verified boot, chart generation, the judgement rail and health indicator through their real event paths, but did not play a live run with key presses this stage, and did not re-measure FPS.
- NPS targets are treated as caps, not quotas: easy/hard sit below the ceiling on most tracks (e.g. hard 2.54–2.84 vs 3.4) because the density governor only removes filler and never invents notes to hit a number. This is the deliberate "don't destroy accents" deviation.
- Hazard cadence meets 12–18 s for hard/expert (13.1–18.9 s); easy/medium run longer (up to 27.6 s) because hazards come only from strong snares and are dropped rather than forced.
- Real-audio fixtures cover Clementi, Chopin and Brahms only (Mozart/Beethoven not captured). Synthetic cases are labelled `synthetic-120bpm` and validate the generator, not the analyser.
- `offBeatRatio` and `sixteenthRatio` are reported but not asserted: on real recordings they are dominated by onset jitter, so the expert-differentiation assertions rest on NPS, median gap, chord presence and lane flow instead.

Untouched: `BeatMapGenerator.js`, `Scoring.js`, `CHART_VERSION`/PRNG, timing windows, audio engine, camera/tunnel/floor/pads, StageFraming, choreography, silhouette.

## Storage schema

Namespace `neon-pulse:scores:v1`, one JSON payload `{ version: 1, records: { … } }`, keyed `"<trackId>::<difficulty>::v<chartVersion>"` (e.g. `qa-real-clementi::expert::v2`):

```json
{ "trackId": "qa-real-clementi", "difficulty": "expert", "chartVersion": 2,
  "bestScore": 157320, "bestRank": "A", "bestAccuracy": 94.761904…,
  "bestCombo": 206, "bestCounts": { "perfect": 180, "great": 20, "good": 6, "miss": 3, "bomb": 1 },
  "attempts": 4, "lastScore": 98040, "lastPlayed": 1794505990695, "lastAttemptId": "e2e-4" }
```

No audio, no titles, no user data — 12 documented fields, roughly 320 bytes per record. Corrupt payloads are dropped; a blocked localStorage falls back to memory; `setItem` failures are swallowed.

## Example before/after comparison (real browser, real `Game.finish`)

| | Run 1 (first attempt) | Run 4 (worse, failed at 42 %) |
|---|---|---|
| Score | 157,320 | 98,040 |
| Rank / accuracy | A / 94.8 % | B / 81.5 % |
| Combo | 206 | 145 |
| Badge | NEW RECORD | none |
| Delta line | — (first run) | −59,280 VS 157,320 (red) |
| Best line | BEST 157,320 · A · 94.8 % · 206 COMBO · RUN 1 | BEST 157,320 · A · 94.8 % · 206 COMBO · RUN 4 |
| Hint | “First run logged — this is your baseline.” | none (next rank 6.5 % away, so no invented advice) |
| Failure line | — | 42 % COMPLETED · 0:45 / 1:47 |

Replaying the same results event (`attemptId e2e-1` twice) left attempts at 1 and did not rewrite storage; a genuinely new attempt counted normally.

## Prompt tiếp theo: Stage 4.4 — Dancer Reactivity

Đây là bước mình muốn ưu tiên tiếp theo: khiến dancer phản ứng trực quan hơn với cách người chơi đánh nhịp.

Writing

# Stage 4.4 — Responsive Dancer & Gameplay Expression

Continue from the accepted Stage 4.3 baseline.

Implement Stage 4.4 directly in the existing codebase.

The goal is to make the existing white silhouette dancer visibly react to player performance without replacing the existing choreography system.

## 1\. Audit first

Read:

- `src/game/DanceSilhouette.js`
- `src/game/DanceChoreography.js`
- `src/core/Game.js`
- `src/game/Scoring.js`
- Existing judgement event payloads
- Existing section/drop data flow
- Existing seek and restart handling

Understand the current animation pipeline before editing.

## 2\. Lane-aware reactions

Currently, the dancer reacts to judgement strength but does not adequately distinguish which lane was hit.

Add lane-aware accent behavior:

- Lane 0: leftward body emphasis.
- Lane 1: subtle left-centre emphasis.
- Lane 2: subtle right-centre emphasis.
- Lane 3: rightward body emphasis.

The reaction must be visually readable but restrained.

Avoid simply translating the entire silhouette off-centre. Prefer a combination of:

- Torso lean
- Shoulder rotation
- Hip counter-shift
- Arm emphasis

Left and right reactions should be mirror-symmetric.

## 3\. Combo-tier expression

Make dancer accent intensity reflect combo tiers.

Suggested tiers:

- Combo 1–9: subtle reaction.
- Combo 10–24: medium reaction.
- Combo 25–49: strong reaction.
- Combo 50+: expressive reaction.

Higher combos should feel more energetic without breaking the existing pose proportions.

Use smooth interpolation. Avoid sudden animation jumps.

## 4\. Miss recovery

A miss should create a distinct but controlled response:

- Brief recoil.
- Small shoulder drop.
- Short recovery over approximately one beat.
- Return smoothly to the normal song-driven choreography.

Do not permanently alter the pose or accumulate offsets.

## 5\. Drop / section expression

During a drop or climax:

- Increase choreography emphasis.
- Use more expressive existing movement families.
- Add restrained accent intensity.

Do not replace the current seven movement families or introduce uncontrolled random animation.

## 6\. Determinism — critical

All added animation must be reproducible.

Requirements:

- Same song time + BPM + seed + section + gameplay event history must produce the same pose.
- Seeking backwards and forwards must reproduce identical poses.
- Pause must freeze the pose.
- Restart must clear transient reactions.
- No `Math.random()`.
- No animation behavior that depends on accumulated frame-by-frame offsets.

Use song-time-derived envelopes for accents and recovery.

If event history is needed, store timestamped events and derive their influence from current song time.

## 7\. Safety and anatomy

Preserve:

- Existing joint hierarchy.
- Arm-length limits.
- Leg-length limits.
- Grounded support foot.
- Full-body panel fitting.
- Existing silhouette rendering style.

Test at least 500 sampled poses across slow, medium and fast BPM.

Check:

- Finite joint coordinates.
- No limb-length violations.
- No joint clipping.
- No foot drift.
- No abrupt discontinuities.

## 8\. Visual QA

Capture:

- Lane 0 vs lane 3 mirrored reactions.
- All four combo tiers.
- Miss recoil and recovery.
- Normal section vs drop/climax.
- Existing seven movement families.

Use the actual silhouette renderer, not a separate mockup.

## 9\. Preservation rules

Do not redesign:

- Neon tunnel.
- Camera.
- Reflective floor.
- Pads.
- Four-lane mapping.
- Judgement rail.
- Results screen.
- ScoreStore.
- BeatMapGenerator.
- Audio engine.
- Scoring formulas.

Only touch files needed for dancer behavior and the minimum required integration.

## 10\. Git instructions

Check Git status first.

Do not:

- Run git init.
- Create a repository.
- Create remotes.
- Push.
- Publish.
- Commit unless explicitly instructed.

I will create the repository and handle pushing myself.

## 11\. Final report

Include:

- Files changed.
- Lane reaction measurements.
- Combo tier intensity measurements.
- Miss recovery duration.
- Drop behavior.
- 500+ pose validation.
- Seek determinism results.
- Build and regression results.
- Screenshots or animation clips.
- Honest limitations.

Stop after Stage 4.4. Do not start Stage 4.5.

## Files changed

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `src/game/DanceExpression.js` | **new**, 377 lines — all reaction math: lane emphasis, combo tiers, judgement strengths, hit envelopes, one-beat miss window, drop boost, and `applyExpression()` (rigid pose transforms only) |

## Lane reaction measurements (120 BPM, seed 7, combo 30)

Joint deltas against the same frame with no hit — head / chest / hips (metres):

| Lane | Head Δx | Chest Δx | Hips Δx | Shoulder-line Δy |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | −0.0347 | −0.0130 | **+0.0225** | +0.0905 |
| 1 | −0.0118 | −0.0044 | **+0.0077** | +0.0260 |
| 2 | +0.0118 | +0.0044 | **−0.0077** | −0.0260 |
| 3 | +0.0346 | +0.0131 | **−0.0226** | −0.0904 |

Read: torso leans toward the hit lane, shoulders roll with it, and the hips shift the *opposite* way (weight shift, not a slide). No translation of the silhouette — the panel still auto-centres the whole body.

Mirror symmetry is exact: applying lane *n* to a mirrored pose reproduces the mirrored result with **0.0** deviation over all 21 joints for every lane pair.

Rendered check on the actual 2D canvas (168×232 panel, bright-pixel mask, head-band vs hip-band centroid relative to the base frame): lane 0 **−4.69 px**, lane 1 −1.60, lane 2 +1.84, lane 3 **+4.89 px** — ordered, mirrored, and restrained (≈4 px of head displacement in a 168 px panel).

## Combo tiers (lane 3)

| Tier | Combo | Scale | Lean | Shoulder roll | Arm lift | Head shift | Wrist shift | Rendered tilt |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| subtle | 0–9 | 0.50 | 0.063 rad (3.6°) | 0.056 rad | 0.083 rad | 21.8 mm | 92 mm | +2.90 px |
| medium | 10–24 | 0.75 | 0.094 (5.4°) | 0.083 | 0.125 | 32.6 mm | 138 mm | +3.55 px |
| strong | 25–49 | 1.00 | 0.125 (7.2°) | 0.111 | 0.167 | 43.5 mm | 182 mm | +4.89 px |
| expressive | 50+ | 1.30 | 0.163 (9.3°) | 0.145 | 0.217 | 56.4 mm | 234 mm | +6.45 px |

Monotonic in every channel; intensities are multipliers on the same envelopes, so tier changes interpolate continuously rather than switching animation.

## Miss recovery

Envelope is a finite sine window, `sin(π·Δ/1.0)` beats: 0 at the hit, peak 0.32 at 0.50 beat, **exactly 0 at 1.00 beat** (0.5 s at 120 BPM, 0.469 s at 128 BPM). At the peak the body recoils 51.9 mm and the shoulder line drops 0.056 rad (3.2°) — the rendered tile is 12 px shorter than the base frame (159 vs 171 px). After the window the pose is bit-identical to the choreography frame: residual lean and miss are exactly 0, no accumulated offset.

## Drop / climax

The drop section applies a smoothstep section-age boost of **1.32** over 2 beats plus a slow beat-locked sway `sin(beat·τ/4)`. Same seven families, same events: head shift 38.2 → 47.8 mm (+25 %), wrist travel 188.4 → 243.5 mm (+29 %), rendered tilt +4.89 → +6.74 px.

## 500+ pose validation

600 samples × {70, 120, 175} BPM × seeded sections: `maxBoneDrift 0` (every `POSE_EDGES` bone length identical to the mm), `maxSupportDrift 0` (support ankle restored bit-exactly, 285 left / 315 right), `minJointGap 0.0306 m` (no joint clipping), all joint coordinates finite, all seven families exercised (`groove, upper, shoulders, arms, hips, legs, turn`).

Continuity: worst frame-to-frame delta with expression **0.1317 m** versus **0.1323 m** without it — the layer adds nothing beyond the choreography's own fastest arm gesture (leftWrist at 10.15 s). Miss frames peak at 0.0731 m/frame. Exponential hit tails are tapered to zero inside a 4-beat window so an accent can never step off when its event leaves the history.

## Determinism

Same song time + BPM + section + event history → same pose hash; paused frames identical (verified both in Node and by comparing two `toDataURL()` captures of the real canvas); seek backward/forward reproduces the identical pose (`seekBackPose` matches); event insertion is order-independent (binary insert, capped at 28); restart clears transients (0 events, lean 0, miss 0). No `Math.random()` anywhere in the new module, and no value is carried over from the previous frame — every term is `f(songTime − event.time)`.

Integration path verified through the real event bus (`game:judge` → `Game` → `DanceSilhouette.accent`): lane 2 perfect at combo 33 → lane 0.34, tier `strong`, lean −0.045; a miss → 0.318 at peak then 0; restart → 0.

## Build & regressions

`npm run build` **0** · `qa/stage44-dancer-check.mjs` **0 failures** · `qa/stage42-chart-check.mjs` **0** · `qa/stage43-score-store-check.mjs` **0** · `qa/stage36-layout-check.mjs` **0** (desktop layout preserved) · `qa/validate-exports.mjs` **0** (export smoke passes).

## Library and selection

- **Continue card** at the top of the library: newest playable recent track, its difficulty, BPM, duration and stored best (`BEST 15,230 · S · 98.2%`), plus a full-width CONTINUE button that starts it immediately. Hidden when nothing is playable (an upload that expired with the session is skipped).
- **Favourites**: star per row (`aria-pressed`, gold left edge), `★ FAVES n` filter chip in the toolbar, persisted.
- **Recents**: one entry per track, newest first, capped at 8, rendered as a horizontal strip; entries whose track is gone are dimmed and disabled.
- **BPM + duration in every row**: `128 BPM · 1:07 · EDM · BUILT-IN`; missing values render `BPM AUTO` and `—:—` instead of a fake number (Jamendo/local tracks carry `bpm: null` until analysis).
- **Recommended difficulty** from ScoreStore records: anchors on the hardest tier actually played, promotes at ≥97 % accuracy, demotes below 70 %, never past easy/expert. It preselects the row's difficulty, is marked gold when it differs from the chosen chip, and is labelled `REC NORMAL` in the stats column.
- **One screen**: difficulty chips, star and PLAY live in the row, so starting a track is a single tap; clicking the title still opens the original difficulty detail screen, so the old flow and the results-screen "CHANGE TRACK" path are intact.
- Upload flow, drop zone, demo track and Jamendo integration are untouched; the notice for a missing client id still renders.

## Mobile polish

- **Pad press feedback**: the pressed touch column lights with a theme-coloured glow (`opacity 1` + radial gradient) while held; pressed state is tracked per pointer id, so two fingers stay lit independently.
- **Haptics**: perfect 12 ms, great 8 ms, good 5 ms, miss `[26, 36, 26]` double pulse, bomb `[40, 40, 40, 40, 70]` warning. Optional via the existing Settings toggle, silent when `navigator.vibrate` is missing or rejects, duplicates within 90 ms suppressed. The old ad-hoc vibrations in `UIManager` (12 ms on perfect, 8 ms on every pointerdown) are gone, so a hit no longer buzzes twice.
- **Fullscreen** button in the pause panel, hidden on unsupported browsers, label flips to EXIT FULLSCREEN, listens to `fullscreenchange`/`webkitfullscreenchange`.
- **Portrait hint**: a small non-blocking pill (container `pointer-events: none`, only the × is interactive) shown once per install on coarse-pointer portrait 360–820 px during gameplay, auto-hides after 7 s, dismissal persisted.

Preserved as required: camera/StageFraming, world geometry, pad geometry and colours, four-lane mapping (`A/S/D/F → 0/1/2/3` verified), judgement/scoring formulas, timing windows, audio sync, BeatMapGenerator, DanceChoreography/DanceExpression, ScoreStore schema and records (verified byte-identical payload after library writes), results screen.

## Known limitations

- **No physical-device testing.** Everything mobile was Chrome viewport emulation at 360/390/430/1280; real vibration output, iOS behaviour and true multi-touch hardware delivery were not exercised.
- `pointer: coarse` is false on this desktop Chrome, so the portrait-hint path was triggered with a `matchMedia` stub in-page (the predicate itself is covered by unit tests); the pad-touch band appears by width, so the touch layout is real.
- The browser panel is occluded (`document.hidden === true`), so GSAP entry tweens never finish and CSS transitions freeze. Screenshots forced the settled state (`visibility/opacity !important`, `transition: none`); the layout captured is the app's real computed layout, but the entry animation and pad-glow fade were not observed running.
- Screenshot capture is fixed at 480 px wide regardless of the resize request, so no genuine 1280-wide image exists; desktop is verified by computed layout only. No video recording was produced for the same reason.
- Jamendo is not configured, so the lists held one real track; the favourites/recent/continue/unavailable-entry paths were exercised with the demo track plus one synthetic recent entry.

# Stage 4.6 — Dynamic Dance Floor & Forward Motion

Implement a subtle, continuous forward-motion effect for the NEON PULSE rhythm game.

## 1\. Objective

The current dance floor appears static. It should create the illusion that the player is moving forward through a futuristic neon tunnel.

The desired visual behavior:

- The dance floor slowly scrolls toward the player.
- Notes and bombs travel from the far end of the tunnel toward the player.
- The tunnel itself remains visually stable.
- Dance pads remain fixed at their existing judgement positions.

The result should feel like a real 3D rhythm game, not a static scene with objects simply appearing and disappearing.

## 2\. Mandatory audit before implementation

Read and understand:

- `src/world/` — floor, tunnel, lighting, materials and world updates.
- `src/objects/NoteManager.js`
- `src/core/Game.js`
- `src/audio/MusicPlayer.js`
- `src/world/StageFraming.js`
- `src/world/DancePads.js`

Identify:

1. How the floor grid is currently rendered.
2. Whether the floor uses a texture, shader, repeated geometry or vertex animation.
3. How note positions are calculated and updated.
4. How the judgement position is determined.
5. How the game handles pause, resume, restart and seeking.
6. Which existing animation systems already use song time.

Do not start changing code until the current movement architecture is understood.

## 3\. Dance floor animation

Implement a subtle continuous forward-scrolling effect.

Requirements:

- The floor grid must move toward the player.
- Use a slow, smooth and consistent speed.
- The movement must create a clear sense of depth and forward travel.
- Avoid excessive speed, flashing, camera shake or distracting visual effects.
- Preserve the existing floor geometry, colours, materials and overall appearance.
- Do not change the camera position, FOV or tunnel geometry.
- Do not move the physical dance pads.

The animation should remain subtle enough that it does not distract from hitting notes.

## 4\. Notes and bombs movement

Preserve the existing note travel system wherever possible.

Verify that:

- Notes spawn at the far end of the tunnel.
- Notes travel toward the player.
- Their apparent speed increases naturally as they approach, through existing perspective projection.
- Notes reach the exact existing judgement position.
- Bombs follow the same travel direction and timing model.
- No change is made to scoring, timing windows, hit detection or chart timestamps.

Do not introduce a second independent movement system if the existing NoteManager already handles this correctly.

## 5\. Synchronization

This is critical.

Prefer a song-time-derived animation:

`phase = songTime * scrollSpeed`

Do not rely exclusively on accumulated frame delta.

The floor must:

- Freeze when paused.
- Resume at the correct phase.
- Return to its initial phase on restart.
- Seek to the corresponding phase when the song is seeked.
- Remain consistent across 30, 60 and 120 FPS.
- Avoid drift between audio and visual movement.

Use the existing MusicPlayer clock as the authoritative time source.

If a separate visual clock is necessary, document why.

## 6\. Responsive and performance requirements

Test at:

- 360 × 800
- 390 × 844
- 430 × 932
- 1280 × 720

Preserve the accepted portrait and desktop compositions.

Performance targets:

- Maintain approximately 60 FPS.
- No additional expensive draw calls if avoidable.
- No unnecessary geometry creation every frame.
- No memory leaks or accumulating animation objects.
- No WebGL errors.

## 7\. Strictly protected systems

Do NOT modify:

- Camera position or FOV.
- StageFraming calculations.
- DancePads geometry, size or position.
- Four-lane mapping.
- Note judgement depth.
- BeatMapGenerator.
- Scoring formulas.
- Timing windows.
- Audio playback or synchronization logic.
- DanceExpression or DanceChoreography.
- Stage 4.1 feedback HUD.
- Stage 4.3 ScoreStore.
- Stage 4.5 Library and mobile UX.

Only modify the minimum files required for the floor animation and its integration.

## 8\. Verification

Create:

`qa/stage46-motion-check.mjs`

Verify:

1. Floor phase changes during playback.
2. Floor phase remains unchanged while paused.
3. Resume continues from the correct phase.
4. Restart resets the floor phase.
5. Seeking updates the floor phase correctly.
6. The animation produces identical phases at different FPS for the same song time.
7. Note judgement positions remain unchanged.
8. Desktop and portrait framing remain unchanged.
9. Existing gameplay regression suites still pass.
10. Build succeeds.

Capture screenshots or a short gameplay video demonstrating:

- Static initial state.
- Floor movement during playback.
- Notes approaching the pads.
- Portrait gameplay.
- Desktop gameplay.

If the browser is backgrounded or rendering is suspended, do not claim live animation verification. Bring the tab to the foreground and retry.

## 9\. Git policy — IMPORTANT

Do NOT:

- Run `git init`.
- Create a new Git repository.
- Configure a remote.
- Push or publish anything.
- Commit unless a Git repository already exists and the user has explicitly authorized it.

The user will create the repository and push manually.

If there is no Git repository, leave all changes as ordinary workspace files and report that no commit was created.

## 10\. Final report

Provide:

- Files changed and why.
- Explanation of the floor movement implementation.
- Explanation of synchronization with song time.
- Measured movement speed.
- FPS before and after.
- All test results.
- Screenshots/video paths.
- Confirmation that protected systems were not modified.
- Known limitations.

Do not begin Stage 4.7 or any unrelated work.

**Priority: natural, slow forward motion with perfect synchronization and zero regression to the existing gameplay experience.**

Lưu ý: Prompt này cố tình yêu cầu AI kiểm tra NoteManager trước. Notes có thể đã bay đúng hướng rồi, vấn đề chính có thể chỉ nằm ở sàn đang thiếu animation. Không nên sửa một hệ thống đang chạy đúng chỉ để tạo hiệu ứng chuyển động.



Starting Stage 4.6 with the audit — reading the world/floor, pads, note travel and the song clock.

Audit complete: the floor is a single shader plane in `NeonTunnel` (ripples on wall-clock time, no directional scroll), notes are already pure `songTime` functions, and `Game.lastSongTime` is the authoritative visual clock. Implementing now.








































Stage 4.6 is implemented, wired, and verified. Final report below.

## Files changed

| File | Change | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `src/world/FloorMotion.js` **(new, 74 lines)** | Pure module: `floorScroll`, `floorRungPhase`, `floorPatchOffset` + constants (`FLOOR_SCROLL_SPEED 1.35` m/s, `FLOOR_RUNG_SPACING 2.6` m, `FLOOR_LENGTH 270`, `FLOOR_PATCH_REPEAT_Y 3.2`, `FLOOR_RUNG_SECONDS 1.926`) | One testable definition of the phase; no three.js, no DOM, no timers |
| `src/world/NeonTunnel.js` (+46/−4) | `uScroll` uniform on the floor and reflection-streak shaders; travelling rung train + slower outer-deck train in the floor fragment; drifting wet-patch texture offset; `update(..., scroll = 0)` | The floor is a single shader plane, so travel is a uniform — no geometry, no new draw calls |
| `src/world/NeonWorld.js` (+10/−1) | Accepts `songTime`, stores `this.floorScroll`, forwards `scroll` to `tunnel.update`, clears on `reset()` | Keeps the world contract and makes the phase introspectable |
| `src/core/Game.js` (+11/−1) | Passes `songTime: this.lastSongTime` into `world.update` | The floor reads the same music clock the silhouette and notes already read |
| `src/studio/StudioStage.js` (+1) | Passes its existing `songTime` to the same call | Studio preview shows the same floor, no second code path |
| `qa/stage46-motion-check.mjs` **(new)** | 133 checks (phase maths, pause/seek/restart/FPS, wiring, notes, framing, protected-file scan) | The required verification suite |
| `qa-artifacts/stage46-motion-check.json` | Generated report | Machine-readable results |

## How the floor moves

The floor is one `PlaneGeometry(80, 270)` mesh whose fragment shader builds the neon grid. I did not touch its geometry, colours, alpha or the walkway/wall/beam layers. The only addition is one float uniform, `uScroll`, holding **metres travelled**:

```glsl
float metres     = y * 270.0;                               // 0 at the near edge
float rungPhase  = mod(metres + uScroll, 2.6);              // 2.6 m rungs
float rungLine   = 1.0 - smoothstep(0.0, 0.14, min(rungPhase, 2.6 - rungPhase));
```

Because `y` decreases toward the camera, *adding* `uScroll` makes each rung slide toward the player — rungs enter at the far end and pass under the pads. Three further cues ride on the same value so the whole floor reads as one surface: a second train on the outer deck at half rate (`uScroll * 0.5`), the wet-patch texture offset (`floorPatchTexture.offset.y`), and the reflection streaks (`uScroll / 270`). Intensity stays low (rung contribution ≈0.16 cyan, deck ≈0.045), faded in only outside the sliver of floor hidden under the pads, so the lane area where you read notes stays calm.

Cost: **zero new meshes, materials, textures or draw calls** — one uniform write per material per frame. Measured scene: 48 draw calls, 6,498 triangles, floor still a single mesh.

## Synchronisation

```js
phase = songTime * 1.35        // metres, straight from the transport
```

`songTime` is `Game.lastSongTime`, which is written from `MusicPlayer.getTime()` (latency-compensated) only while playing, is left untouched by `pause()`, is assigned by `seek()`, and is zeroed by `startSong()`/restart. Nothing accumulates `dt` — `NeonWorld.time += dt` still drives the ambient ripples, but the travel term never reads it. **No separate visual clock exists**, so there is nothing to keep in sync: pause freezes the grid because the clock stops, a seek jumps the phase because the clock jumps, restart returns to phase 0, and identical song times give bit-identical phases at any frame rate.

Suite evidence: paused phase identical across 300 frames; resume continues with no jump; seeks to 40 s and back to 2 s produce exactly 54 m and 2.7 m (no accumulated distance); restart = 0; 30/60/120 FPS runs agree within 1e-9 m and 1e-9 phase, including ±65 % jittered frame times; 10 minutes of 60 FPS steps equal the closed form (no drift).

## Measured motion

- Speed **1.35 m/s (81 m/min)**; one rung passes every **1.926 s**; 1.755 m per 0.65× speed unit.
- Pixel proof with the wall clock frozen (`dt = 0`) so only song time differs, songTime 0 → 1.3 s: floor band **11.0 %** of pixels changed (mean 3.1/255, peaks 35–53/255); upper tunnel region **0.00 %** changed (mean 0.009) — the tunnel is stable, only the floor travels.
- Notes are 22× faster (30 m/s), verified unchanged: a note at its chart time sits exactly on the framing `hitZ` (6.26494 portrait, 0 desktop), travel distance is exactly `speed × Δtime`, bombs share the model, lead time `(hitZ − SPAWN_Z)/speed`.

## FPS

I could **not** sample live frame rate: this preview tab is occluded (`document.hidden === true`) and Chromium delivered **0 requestAnimationFrame callbacks per second**, so the game loop itself is suspended — there is no live frame pacing here to measure, before or after. What I measured instead is the synchronous cost of the full pipeline with `gl.finish()` at a 1280×720 buffer: **2.42 ms/frame with travel vs 2.21 ms/frame with the scroll parked** (~+0.2 ms, within single-run noise; implied ceiling ≈412 fps). The added shader work is a few ALU ops in an existing fragment shader plus one float assignment per frame.

## Known limitations
